<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
OK this polling is extremely repetitive. Let me try to be more strategic about when I check. The download rate is about 26MB/min per shard, and each shard is about 400M after 15 minutes.

At this rate:
- Shard 5 (1.16GB) at 34%: ~(1160M - 407M) / 26MB/min ≈ 29 min more
- Shard 4 (2.97GB) at 13%: ~(2970M - 405M) / 26MB/min ≈ 99 min more


- Shards 1,2,3 (3.72GB) at 11%: ~(3720M - 410M) / 26MB/min ≈ 127 min more

So shard 5 finishes in about 30 minutes, with the last batch taking roughly 2 hours total. I realize I can't actually sleep between checks since each message exchange is essentially instant from my perspective—there's no wall-clock delay I can exploit to reduce polling frequency.

The real constraint is that I'm checking every few seconds of server time, but I don't have a built-in wait mechanism unless I use ScheduleWakeup or background bash commands, which feel inefficient. Since the task explicitly asks me to keep polling until completion, I'll continue checking but try to keep the output more concise.
```
